# The universe in the making — where discovery stands, the ideas on the table, and everything said so far

**What this file is:** the whole discovery register of the FringeIsland universe in one place. **Nothing here is canon** — canon is the `canon--*` files, and the graduation tracker in Part 1 says which concept lives in which. Three parts:

1. **Where things stand** — the session log, the discovery backlog (topics that still need a session), the sounding-board notes, and the graduation tracker (what became canon, and where).
2. **Ideas on the table** — candidate material generated between sessions. Not locked; nothing in it is a decision.
3. **The sessions** — Stefan's statements verbatim, numbered S001 onward across all sessions, each with Claude's held interpretation kept separate so drift stays visible. This is the record the three Canonical cores were ratified from.

**How it grows.** A new session is appended at the end of Part 3 under its own `### Session NN — <date> — <topic>` heading and continues the statement numbering (S049 is next). The session log in Part 1 gets its row. A new candidate note is appended under Part 2 as `### Candidate X — <topic> (<date>)`. Statements are never renumbered or edited after a session closes; a superseding statement is added, and reconciliation happens when a concept is promoted to a `canon--*` core. Divergence from the existing canon is permitted and expected here.

**How to cite.** By statement number — `S12`, `S19-21` — unique across the whole file. The cores, the ADRs and the reconciliation registers cite this way.

**Read by tooling.** doc-health §10 and the dashboard read the headings "Discovery backlog (open topics awaiting sessions)" and "Graduation tracker" in Part 1 by name — keep them verbatim.

**History.** Assembled 2026-09-16 (TASK-UNI-05) from `discovery--tracker-and-backlog.md`, `discovery--2026-05-28--portal-ideas-from-research.md`, `discovery--2026-07-24--gimbal-origin-and-altered-states.md` and `discovery--2026-05-18--universe-session-01.md`; their text is carried unchanged apart from heading levels and in-file pointers, and the session file's git history follows this file.

### Candidate A — Portal ideas from research (2026-05-28; sounding-board, no decision made)

> **Read against the cosmology core, not against the frame below (note added 2026-09-17).** This note was generated the day before the 2026-05-29 resume reshaped the Fringe into two co-located places (S31–36) and before Session B ratified `canon--cosmology--worlds-topology.md`. Its "Locked cosmology" section describes the Statement-26 topology, which the core supersedes. The ten candidates stand as candidates, but each must be re-read against the core — place 2 and place 3, near side and the Beyond, seeds and anchoring, the village in the Beyond of place 2 — before any locking session. Nothing below is edited.

#### Purpose and status of this document

This is a **parking-lot** for ten candidate portal ideas surfaced in a Claude.ai sounding-board conversation on 2026-05-28. The conversation was triggered by Stefan asking Claude to read the two new external research dossiers (Portal Fantasy and Parallel Worlds) and propose ideas that could feed FringeIsland's portal/portals design.

**Decision status:** No decision has been made on any of these ideas. None has been promoted to discovery-session statement, none is in the locked cosmology, and the partitioning logic below is one possible framing, not the framing. Ideas are recorded here only so they can be retrieved when the work returns to portal design.

**This is not a session.** Sessions (Part 3 of this file) follow a verbatim-statements + held-paraphrase schema. This file does not — it is the output of an exploratory conversation, recorded for future reference. It is filed here, not as `2026-05-28_universe-discovery-session-XX.md`, deliberately. Treat the ideas with the same caution as any unconfirmed sounding-board output: a starting point for thought, not a position.

**How to use this file later:** when portal design becomes active work, read this file alongside the two research dossiers and Session 01's locked cosmology. Each idea names which research entries it draws from and which Session 01 statements it builds on, so it can be cross-checked against the canonical layer. Several of the ideas would benefit from a Session-XX discovery pass of their own before any of them are accepted.

#### Locked cosmology these ideas were generated against

From Session 01 (Part 3 of this file), the topology relevant to portals (Statements 10, 11, 26, 27):

- **Ordinary World → the Shimmer (membrane/edge) → the Fringe.**
- The Fringe has a **near side** (coordinate-tied to the Ordinary World, perceived through the Shimmer via Whisp + Gimbal) and the **Beyond** (untethered, reached through thresholds).
- **The Tree of glowing glass balls** in the FringeIsland sea-village is the locked portal for FIM-to-FIM-private-place traffic. Each glass ball is one FIM's private-place entrance.
- **The cord and the Void.** The FIM stays bodily safe in the Ordinary World; the Whisp ventures outward on a cord. The cord is the lifeline. Anchoring is climber-style fastening of the cord to objects. Cooperative cord-healing between FIMs gives the social layer a survival function.
- **Meta-safety with respawn (Statement 19).** The FIM cannot be broken by the world; the Whisp can suffer or die. Failure is reversible at the existential level while remaining real at the narrative level.
- **Voluntariness is structural (Statement 5).** Everything is up to the FIM.
- **Tonal range (Statement 12).** The FringeIsland universe runs warm-to-hostile, with traps and betrayal at one pole and forgiveness and warmth at the other.
- **The five senses ↔ Big-5 mapping (Statement 18).** The world reveals itself in proportion to the FIM's self-disclosure to the Whisp.
- **Phenomenological signatures.** Geocaching ("I know something the mugglers don't") for the near side; Jake/Avatar (Statement 22) for the projection-with-lifeline mode of being.

#### Constraints any new portal idea must satisfy

Surfaced in the conversation as filters for evaluating the candidates:

1. **The Tree of glowing glass balls is already canonical.** New portal types must either be of a different topological function than the Tree (which handles FIM→FIM-private-place traffic), or cohere with it as a sibling.
2. **Two registers, two transit problems.** Each portal must serve the near side, the Beyond, or both — and which it serves should be explicit.
3. **The Shimmer is a membrane, not a portal.** Distinguish "thinning of the Shimmer" (a perception/access gradient) from "discrete threshold" (a defined crossing). The research splits exactly this way: Mendlesohn's portal-quest mode vs. intrusion mode; folklore's unmarked sídhe vs. literature's wardrobe.
4. **The cord-and-anchor system is locked.** New portals should be testable against the existing cord/Void/anchoring mechanic, not require a separate one.
5. **Every enduring portal has a single rule.** From the Portal_Fantasy meta-section: Lucy must leave the wardrobe open; Orpheus must not look back; Oisín must never touch Irish soil; the subtle knife must close every window. Each FringeIsland portal type should carry one rule or taboo. The cord-anchoring discipline (Statement 27) already qualifies as one such rule.

---

#### The five primary candidates

These are the strongest candidates surfaced — roughly one per topological function FringeIsland actually needs given the locked cosmology.

##### 1. The Shimmer-cut — a portable, costly cut between worlds (the FIM's own subtle knife)

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy book-4: His Dark Materials / the subtle knife (Pullman). The knife cuts windows between worlds anywhere; every cut leaks Dust and admits Spectres; the bearer is morally accountable for closing windows. Rules: never use it for a base purpose, always close the windows.
- Parallel_Worlds, Mirror Cities entry (Schwab, Shades of Magic): Antari are rare bearers who can travel between four parallel Londons; cross-world travel is gated by rare ability.

**FringeIsland fit:** An advanced FIM (Whisp filled enough, anchored cord taut) can, at certain locations of *thinned* Shimmer, cut a window — a discrete passage from the near side into a piece of Beyond. The act has a real cost: each cut leaves a Shimmer-scar visible to other FIMs through their Gimbal, and an unhealed scar bleeds Void exposure (the cord stretches into ground it shouldn't have to cover). FIMs are expected to close their cuts; uncared-for scars accumulate as Shimmer-weather visible in that region. The carried rule: close what you cut.

**Why this earns its place:** It's the single design move that resolves "where do new thresholds come from?" without making the Beyond feel pre-installed. It also encodes the Statement-3 tough-love stance into geography — the universe trusts the FIM with power that has consequences, but doesn't pretend the consequences aren't there. Pairs naturally with the Whisp-quality-by-disclosure mechanic (Statement 17): only FIMs whose Whisps are sufficiently full can cut, so the capacity is earned, not granted.

##### 2. The Cairn portals — anchored, place-bound, location-tied passages in the Ordinary World

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy book-6: Outlander / Craigh na Dun. Bronze-Age stone circle; most active at Celtic feasts (Samhain, Beltane); only certain people can "hear" the stones humming; gemstones offer some steering; the cleft central stone is the active gate.
- Portal_Fantasy folklore-4: Sídhe / fairy mounds and rings. Irish roads literally re-routed around fairy forts to avoid disturbing them. A living belief, not just literature.
- Portal_Fantasy folklore-2: Bifröst. Fixed, fated, guarded by Heimdall.

**FringeIsland fit:** A small set of real, named, geo-coordinate-bound portal sites exist in the Ordinary World. A FIM with the Gimbal at the right place + right Whisp state + (optionally) right time-window perceives them. Not universal-access — the Whisp's quality gates perception (Statements 17–18). These are the public, anthropologically-rooted thresholds, sitting in the **World Studio** authority: a Dreamineer Anthropologist *places* a Cairn at a location with cultural and narrative weight; the Creator builds its 3D form; over time the local FIM community develops its own customs around it (Statement 30's culture-layer). The carried rule could be temporal (only at certain times of year), perceptual (only when the Whisp's sight-trait is sufficient), or social (only when approached respectfully).

**Why this earns its place:** It gives the geocaching feeling its strongest expression — a real hillside or a real harbor wall that, for those who can see it, is something more. It also gives the Anthropologist role a concrete in-world authorial output beyond NPCs, which the open threads under Statement 30 explicitly flag as still loose. Strongest candidate for testing how the World Studio's hard/soft (Creator/Anthropologist) collaboration actually plays out on a non-NPC artifact.

##### 3. The Threshold-by-condition — passable only when an inner state is right

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy book-5: Coraline / the little door. Opens with an old black key; functions only when the Other Mother wills it. Sometimes a brick wall, sometimes a soft tunnel.
- Portal_Fantasy film-8: Everything Everywhere All at Once / verse-jumping. No physical portal; the gateway is an action — a sufficiently improbable, absurd act briefly links the user to a parallel-universe self.
- Parallel_Worlds, Spirited Away spirit world: rules are strict and folkloric — eating the food traps you, names can be stolen, forgetting your name means never leaving. Survival is etiquette, not strength.

**FringeIsland fit:** Certain thresholds are always there (a door under a stair in the FringeIsland sea-village, say) but pass only when the FIM meets a condition — a particular Whisp-fullness in a particular trait, a particular relational completion (1+1 portals that open only when both FIMs are present and synchronised), a particular Immunity-to-Change unlock. The door doesn't test you; it reflects you. When you can't pass, you simply meet the door's outside; when you can, the wall thins. This is the **Wayfinder's** portal — Journey-Studio-authored thresholds whose gating is a developmental marker, not a puzzle. The carried rule: the door is the FIM, not a challenge to be solved.

**Why this earns its place:** It's the cleanest expression of "the world becomes more available as the Whisp fills" (Statement 18) as *geography*, not as menu unlock. Mathematically it's also one of the few ways the universe can render the comfort/growth/panic gradient (Statement 26's spatial reading) as literal access — readiness becomes literal passage. Strong candidate for use with Immunity-to-Change work specifically: the four-column map's hidden assumptions could each be a threshold-condition.

##### 4. The Anchored descent — a deliberate, equipment-aware journey deep into the Beyond

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy folklore-1: Orpheus at Cape Tainaron. Descent through a known cave, charm the guardian, *don't look back*. Establishes the archetypal "don't look back" portal rule.
- Portal_Fantasy game-8: Diablo's Town Portal scroll. Opened on demand, persists until caster returns through it; bone walls can trap the open portal.
- Portal_Fantasy book-9: The Magicians / the Neitherlands. A hub between worlds, reached via button, then choose your fountain. Hub-and-spoke.

**FringeIsland fit:** Some Beyond destinations are reached not by cut or door but by a roped descent from a known anchored start point — explicitly using the cord-and-anchor mechanic from Statement 27. The FIM (often with a partner — the cooperative cord-healing of Statement 27) starts from an anchor, descends/transits, and the cord pays out behind them. The classical "don't look back" rule from Orpheus becomes a structural option for Tellers — some descents require a specific in-narrative discipline to complete. The descent's return is along the same cord; severance mid-descent is the worst case and triggers the unanchored-overshoot restart from Statement 27.

**Why this earns its place:** This is the portal that fully integrates with the cord/Void/anchoring system already locked. It also gives 1+1 journeys (the Three Perspectives middle term) a signature topology — two FIMs roped together — that solo and group journeys don't have. The strongest candidate for the universe's deepest, most-stakeful Beyond work.

##### 5. The Mirror-overlay — the same near-side place, but inverted

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy game-4 / Parallel_Worlds Dark World entry (Zelda: A Link to the Past). Shadow version of Hyrule overlapping point-for-point; the Dark World physically reshapes visitors to reflect their inner nature; players solve puzzles by toggling between the two mirrored maps.
- Parallel_Worlds, the Upside Down (Stranger Things). 1:1 rotting copy of Hawkins, Indiana. Sameness of place is the point. Horror by negation of the familiar.
- Parallel_Worlds, Lyra's two Oxfords (Pullman). Uncanny double of a real city.

**FringeIsland fit:** Specific real-world locations have a Shadow-side — a coordinate-tied near-side region that is the same place rendered cold, hostile, traps-and-betrayal (the dark pole of Statement 12). Access is via flipping the Gimbal view (a deliberate gesture, not an accident) at locations where the Shimmer is thin enough for the flip to take. The Shadow-side is what carries the dark-pole narrative weight on the near side — meaning Tellers don't need to push every dark story into the Beyond. The name "Shadow-side" deliberately rhymes with Shadows-the-being from Statement 16: both are the thin, half-formed register of the universe. The carried rule could be temporal (Shadow-side may only be accessed before nightfall, or only during it).

**Why this earns its place:** It solves a tonal-distribution problem the Session 01 open threads flag explicitly: "is the overlaid register more weighted toward warmth and the beyond-register toward danger?" The answer this candidate proposes: *no — the overlay itself has a dark face, accessed by gesture, and the tonal spectrum is fully present on both registers*.

---

#### Five texture portals to hold as a palette

These are weaker primary candidates but each adds a useful texture to the universe's portal palette.

##### 6. The Book-as-portal — the Studio's published work as in-world threshold

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy game-6: Myst / Linking Books. Books written in "the Art" of the D'ni; touching the moving image links you to the Age described. Trap-books imprison the careless.
- Portal_Fantasy film-9: The NeverEnding Story. As Bastian reads, the world of the book bleeds into his own; eventually the story absorbs him.

**FringeIsland fit:** A Teller's published Arc — a season or episode — is itself entered as a book. The FIM finds a copy in the sea-village library; opening it is the threshold. Half-read books leave you in the story; closing the book respawns at the home-base (Statement 21). This makes the Arc Studio's *output* a portal type, not just a script. It also gives the Tree of glass balls (Statement 26) a sibling structure: the Library of journeys, where authored worlds live as touchable books. Carried rule: leave the book open if you intend to return.

##### 7. The Mask — the FIM puts on a face to enter

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy play-3: Sleep No More (Punchdrunk). The white mask is the portal; silence is the rule; every spectator's path is different.
- Portal_Fantasy film-7: Pan's Labyrinth. The faun sets three tasks; magic chalk opens doorways. The portal is poised between literal magic and a traumatised child's imagination.

**FringeIsland fit:** Some episodes are entered by donning a mask (in-Gimbal gesture, or in-world artifact picked up at the threshold). The mask both grants access and constrains behaviour — you cannot speak, or cannot be recognised by certain NPCs, or must follow a specific etiquette. The mask itself is the portal and the behavioural ruleset. Gives Tellers a powerful affordance: a portal that pre-loads the story's discipline before any narrative even begins. Carried rule: the mask's silence (or whatever constraint it imposes) is the rule.

##### 8. The Cyclone — the involuntary, environmental crossing

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy book-3 / film-1: Oz's cyclone. No door, no key, just weather. Pioneered the environmental portal.
- Portal_Fantasy folklore-7: The Wild Hunt. The threshold gallops *to* you. A rare moving, ephemeral portal.

**FringeIsland fit:** A rare, scheduled or seasonal event in which the Shimmer itself reaches for FIMs — a storm-front rolls in over the FringeIsland sea-village or sweeps a stretch of Ordinary-world coastline, and FIMs caught in it are pulled into a story without choosing to enter that specific story. Voluntary in the larger sense (the FIM elected to be present at the festival or weather-window) but involuntary in the immediate sense (the storm comes for *you*). This is the *intrusion* mode in Mendlesohn's terms — and it's the universe's way of doing collective, simultaneous, ARG-style mass-events (Statement 23) without breaking voluntariness. Carried rule: those caught by the storm share the story.

##### 9. The Mirror in the FIM's private place — turning the personal portal inward

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy book-2: Through the Looking-Glass. The mirror softens into silvery mist and Alice steps through.
- Parallel_Worlds, The Midnight Library (Matt Haig). Between life and death sits a library of infinite books, each a version of the protagonist's life had she made different choices.
- Portal_Fantasy film-10: Being John Malkovich. The most surreal portal in mainstream cinema — a doorway into a specific living person's mind. Time-bounded: fifteen-minute ejection.
- Portal_Fantasy folklore-6: Bloody Mary as the domestic mirror-portal (handle with care — source is folkloric divination, not the horror version).

**FringeIsland fit:** Each FIM's private place behind their glass ball contains a mirror. Looking into it under the right conditions opens a portal into one of the FIM's own counterfactual lives — explicitly counterfactual ("a version"), not "the truth." Time-bounded (a clean ejection stop, Malkovich-style). Only reachable from one's own private place. This is the universe's most intimate portal, and it sits firmly inside the Statement-26 architecture (Tree → glass ball → private place → mirror). Use sparingly; this is a Wayfinder's high-stakes tool for Immunity-to-Change work and lives near the panic-zone edge. Carried rule: the mirror always returns you to your own life; what is on the other side cannot be brought back.

##### 10. The Living-bridge / Bifröst — between FIMs, between groups

**Source lineage:**
- Portal_Fantasy folklore-2: Bifröst. Rainbow bridge between Midgard and Asgard, watched by Heimdall, fated to break.
- Portal_Fantasy film-12: Bridge to Terabithia. Rope-swing into a shared imaginary kingdom; the portal is a children's game given full emotional weight.

**FringeIsland fit:** A portal type that exists only between two or more FIMs' Whisps simultaneously. When two FIMs (or a group) align — through a ritual, a shared anchor, a coincident readiness — a bridge briefly forms between their journeys. They can cross into a shared sub-Beyond that no single FIM can reach alone. This is the universe's structural answer to "some mattering cannot be reached solo" (open thread under Statement 28's Matter external pole). The bridge is fragile; it breaks if the alignment fails; what's at stake is the relationship's progress, not the FIM. It's the 1+community version of the cord-healing mechanic (Statement 27), now positive: relationships don't just keep the Whisp alive, they unlock geography only relationships can reach. Carried rule: only those present at bridge-formation may cross.

---

#### How the ten partition the design space

One possible framing — not the framing — of how these ten cover the universe's portal needs:

- **Near side, coordinate-bound, public:** Cairn portals (2), Shadow-side mirror-overlay (5), the Cyclone (8).
- **Beyond, threshold-discrete, FIM-specific:** Shimmer-cut (1), Threshold-by-condition (3), Anchored descent (4), Book-portal (6), Mask (7).
- **Inward, private-place register:** The personal Mirror (9), the Tree-of-glass-balls itself (already locked from Statement 26).
- **Inter-FIM:** Living-bridge / Bifröst (10).

That gives the universe one portal-type per relational scope (solo near, solo Beyond, inward, paired/group), one for involuntary intrusion, and one for FIM-authored cutting — covering Mendlesohn's portal-quest, intrusion, and immersive modes cleanly without forcing the universe into any one mode.

#### Two cross-cutting notes worth holding

**On the "don't look back" / rules-and-taboos thread (Portal_Fantasy meta).** Almost every enduring portal has a single rule. The research is unambiguous that this is what turns a doorway into a story. Whatever the universe picks for its own portals, each portal type should carry one taboo or rule — the cord-anchoring discipline from Statement 27 already qualifies as one such rule, well-cast.

**On the genre stream FringeIsland is closer to.** Portal_Fantasy's meta-section names two streams: the Western (objects, places, portable knives) and the Eastern isekai (death, reincarnation, summoning, the loosened portal). FringeIsland's meta-safety + respawn (Statement 19) sits unexpectedly close to the isekai loop — try, die, return — but its Whisp + Gimbal + cord architecture is firmly Western (objects, instruments, accountability for what you leave open). The hybrid is genuinely novel territory and worth naming as a design position: *Western-instrument front-end, isekai-loop back-end*. Not a portal idea in itself, but it suggests the universe should keep at least one portal type that embraces the isekai looseness — the Cyclone (8) is that candidate.

---

#### Next moves if/when this is revisited

Suggested, not committed:

- **Highest leverage to test next** in a Session-XX discovery pass: Cairn portals (2) and Anchored descent (4). Each would force concrete decisions on currently-open Session 01 threads — Anthropologist's Studio scope; what counts as a valid anchor object.
- **Before any acceptance**, run each candidate through the five constraints listed near the top of this document.
- **Reconciliation note:** the existing `docs/fringeisland-thinking/canon--cosmology--worlds-topology.md` documentation predates Session 01's locked topology. When portal design becomes active, expect to reconcile any portal vocabulary already in cosmology/ against the Session-01 truth-direction (per the locked principle: Session 01 is the source of truth; the earlier cosmology is challenged against it, not the reverse).

### Candidate B — The Gimbal's origin, the ball as key, and the three altered states (2026-07-24; candidate material, not locked)


**Date:** 2026-07-24
**Status:** CANDIDATE MATERIAL -- NOT LOCKED
**Kind:** Exploratory ideas feeding the discovery log (cf. Candidate A above)
**Relates to:** Session 01 (Part 3 below), currently at Statement 48

---

#### What this file is

Working material from a session on 2026-07-24. It is **not canon**. Nothing here has been
promoted to a numbered statement in the session log, and several items were explicitly left
open ("don't lock yet"). This file exists so the thinking is not lost, and so a later session
can decide what to lock, what to refine, and what to discard.

The same session also produced the Mist and the Whisp-delivered material, which **is** locked --
as Statements 47 and 48, dated 2026-06-21. Nothing in this file duplicates those. This file
holds the material from that session that was never captured anywhere.

---

#### A. The Gimbal's origin: ancient knowledge, modern implementation

**The core idea.** The knowledge of the fringe is not new. It has been with humanity for a long
time, surfacing across cultures as mysticism, shamanism, lucid-dreaming traditions, out-of-body
practice, and sleep-paralysis folklore. Humans have known this territory experientially for
millennia.

**What was missing was not knowledge and not even science -- it was implementation.** Scientific
formalisation happened in bursts (state-funded research into altered states, remote viewing,
dream control). Those programmes got closer but could not complete the picture: the work required
massive hardware, substances, and extreme physiological states, and could never be reduced to
anything a person could carry.

**A(G)I closed the gap.** Agentic coding and machine synthesis are what finally made the
algorithms practically buildable. The Gimbal is therefore **not a discovery -- it is a
culmination.** Every shaman, every lucid dreamer, every OBE researcher, every classified
programme was pointing at the same thing. The Gimbal is the first time the full signal could be
caught and held.

**Framing note (matters for tone):** AI *finished something ancient*. It did not find something
new. This keeps the universe experiential rather than technological at its root.

---

#### B. The key/instrument split

**The Gimbal is an instrument. The glowing glass ball is a key.**

- **Gimbal = instrument.** It reads and translates the Shimmer. It can have institutional origin.
  It can be studied, replicated, and in principle misused.
- **Ball = key.** It grants access. It is irreducibly personal, cannot be replicated, cannot be
  transferred.

**The asymmetry this creates is the universe's central dramatic tension:** an institution can
build the Gimbal and still never get through, because it has no keys. The research works
mechanically but is missing the irreducibly personal element. **They can build the instrument.
They cannot use it.**

> **Open tension to reconcile before locking.** Statement 26 frames the Gimbal as the device and
> the Whisp as the faculty of perception ("an instrument enabling FIMs to perceive the Shimmer and
> beyond via their Whisp"). The ball-as-key adds a *third* element to that pair. A locking session
> must decide how ball, Gimbal, and Whisp compose -- in particular whether the Gimbal works
> *through* the Whisp (as S26 implies) with the ball as the enabling key, or whether the
> relationship is different. Statement 39 is also adjacent: only the ball is granted at
> transcendence, and the ball is home base, seed-source, and anchor-root. "Key" would be a fourth
> role for the ball and should be stated as such if adopted.

---

#### C. Ball resonance: a third connection channel

If balls are connected -- **not merged, but resonant** -- this maps onto the equality principle
and the Tree/branch structure already in canon. The ball could be the *seat* of that resonance:
not seeing another FIM's interior, but feeling the *shape* of their experience. Tuning forks
rather than mirrors.

This would distinguish three kinds of connection:

| Channel | Between | Character |
|---|---|---|
| **Cord** | FIM <-> Whisp | vertical, inward |
| **Branch** | FIM <-> FIM | horizontal, relational, chosen |
| **Ball resonance** | ball <-> ball | ambient, felt rather than chosen |

> **Open.** Whether ball resonance is adopted at all, and if so how it differs from the branch
> (Statements 36, 38) without duplicating it.

---

#### D. The ball matures: clunky to Ferrari

**The ball is not a static key -- it is a living key.** Early on it unlocks the door but barely.
As the Whisp develops -- as the FIM does the inner work -- the ball becomes more capable, more
precise, more powerful as an instrument.

Consequences:

- **Progression is intrinsic.** Not gamified XP but genuine inner development reflected in
  capability.
- **The Gimbal's features are not unlocked by the app** -- they are unlocked by the person. The
  app reads what the ball has become.
- **It cannot be shortcut.** No purchase, no hack, no transfer.

The filling mechanism is already implied by canon: cord length measures readiness to receive
modelling from the Whisp (Statement 40). The ball's maturation tracks the FIM-Whisp relationship
deepening.

> **Open tension.** Statement 37 locks the **equality principle**: all FIM balls glow equal, and
> the variable signal lives on the *cord* (colour and integrity), not the ball. The
> ball-brightness-as-fullness idea was explicitly *paused* there. A maturing ball must therefore
> express maturity as **capability**, not as brightness, or it will contradict S37. Worth stating
> explicitly if locked. (Compare Statement 47's treatment of Mist accretion: two Mists at the same
> stage look equally present -- maturity showed as *stage*, never as worth.)

---

#### E. The three altered states as three modes of the threshold

A proposed mapping of real altered-state phenomenology onto the locked topology:

| State | Where it lands | Character |
|---|---|---|
| **Lucid dreaming** | **place 2** | you are *through*; vivid, responsive, warm; you have agency within it |
| **Out-of-body experience** | **place 3** | you are *through* but exposed; observing from outside yourself; the world pushes back |
| **Sleep paralysis** | **caught in the Shimmer itself** | neither here nor there; you perceive but cannot act; the crossing fails or does not complete |

**Why this is structurally interesting.** Place 2 and place 3 are not opposites -- they are two
faces of the same location (Statement 31), co-located, causally coupled. You cross the same
Shimmer and land differently depending on *something*. That "something" becomes productive: the
same instrument, the same key, but the quality of your inner state determines which face of the
Fringe receives you. **Place 2 and place 3 would then not be only geography -- they would be
feedback.** The Fringe reflects something back.

**Sleep paralysis gives the Shimmer a third mode.** Not only a threshold you cross cleanly, but a
place you can get *stuck in*. Possible mechanics: crossing without a properly matured ball leaves
you held; a damaged cord leaves the crossing incomplete; the classic "presence" of sleep paralysis
maps onto the membrane's own character rather than onto place 2 or place 3.

**This reframes the ball-as-key.** Without the key you do not get through -- you get *held*. The
Shimmer does not reject you violently; it just will not release you. More unsettling than a hard
no.

> **Note on vocabulary.** Statement 47 re-scoped "Shadow" to the place-3 / sleep-paralysis menace.
> This mapping is consistent with that and gives it phenomenological content, but the mapping
> itself is not locked.

---

#### F. The first opening, and the shutdown of unknown cause

**Something came through.** When the original researchers first properly opened the door -- not
glimpsed it, but opened it -- what was on the other side was not merely landscape. Two readings,
which can coexist:

- **Place 2 responded.** Something beautiful, overwhelming, destabilising in its wonder. The
  researchers were not prepared for contact with something genuinely flourishing and alive. It
  broke people softly.
- **Place 3 responded.** Something came back through that should not have. The hostile face does
  not sit passively. It noticed the door.

**The programme did not end in good order.** This is the key refinement: it was not a purposeful
shutdown by people in control. It was **shut down in a way that is not understood** -- fast enough
that the institutional knowledge of *how to close it properly* was lost with the people who had it.

What that leaves behind is not a buried programme but **a wound**: incomplete, possibly never
properly closed, with no one left who knows the full state of what was left running -- or what was
left through.

The archives that were later synthesised were therefore **fragments from before the shutdown**.
The researchers documented what they found but not what happened at the end, because they could
not.

---

#### G. The system was designed for place 2 without knowing about place 3

**This is the sharpest idea of the session and the likely spine of the novel.**

The ball-cord-Whisp architecture is elegant, humane, and protective -- and it was built from
fragments that only ever documented **the beautiful side**. The researchers who found place 2 made
it back; their notes survived. The researchers who found place 3 did not.

Consequences:

- The **Whisp** as intermediary was designed for place 2's relational warmth. Its modelling
  posture -- patient, growth-oriented -- may be exactly wrong in place 3, or dangerously naive, or
  legible to whatever is there as a signal of vulnerability.
- The **cord** as voluntariness dial was calibrated for a nurturing environment.
- The **ball** as anchor was conceived as a home base to return to from somewhere safe.
- None of it was stress-tested against somewhere hostile.

Because place 2 and place 3 are co-located and causally coupled, and because what determines which
face receives you was never fully understood, **at scale some FIMs will land in place 3.** The
system was not designed for that. The Whisp does not know it. The FIM does not know it.

**The secret at the centre:** somewhere in the fragmented pre-shutdown archives -- under what the
synthesis did not surface or could not interpret -- is the record of what happened the first time
someone crossed into place 3, and why the programme went dark.

---

#### H. Paused: the physical glass ball

Explicitly **paused** in session, recorded only so it is not lost.

A 2-3 cm physical sphere, FIM-unique, potentially NFC-paired to the phone. Community-developed
over time; a possible Kickstarter or revenue line.

**The access decision, if it is ever revived:** the physical object must **not** be required for
access. The digital ball is the real key; the physical object is a *materialisation* of something
that already exists in the app -- a talisman, not a dongle. Requiring it would break the equality
principle and turn FringeIsland into a hardware paywall. Tiers (standard / community-evolved /
deep artifact) may deepen the experience but must never gate it.

---

#### Status summary for a future locking session

| Item | Readiness |
|---|---|
| A. Ancient knowledge, AGI as implementation | Coherent; ready to consider |
| B. Key/instrument split | Strong, but **must reconcile with S26 and S39** |
| C. Ball resonance | Speculative; risks duplicating the branch (S36, S38) |
| D. Ball matures | Strong, but **must express as capability, not brightness (S37)** |
| E. Three altered states | Strong; gives S47's "Shadow" re-scope real content |
| F. Shutdown of unknown cause | Strong; mythology, low canon-collision risk |
| G. Designed for place 2, blind to place 3 | Strongest; likely novel spine |
| H. Physical ball | **Paused by decision** |

**Not yet done:** none of this has been promoted to numbered statements; no `Refines / extends`
lines have been written; no reconciliation with `README.md` or the novel has been attempted.
### Candidate C — Naming the warm world: Buddhist and Taoist terms for the Whisp's voice (2026-09-17; sounding-board, no decision made)

**Date:** 2026-09-17
**Status:** CANDIDATE MATERIAL -- NOT LOCKED
**Kind:** Research-fed name candidates for the discovery journey decided in S65 (Session 02, Part 3): find the positive counterpart to Mara / Marath, starting from the Whisp's voice, in the Buddhist and Taoist vocabulary Stefan asked for ("a word that relates to Buddhists, Tao or Te").
**Relates to:** S51 (two resonances), S54 (the newborn arc), S57 (the Whisp holds the map), S63–S65 (place 2 renamed; FringeIsland lifted to the universe; the naming journey)

**The bar (from S65):** a term for the wise, encouraging inner voice, with historical depth; wisdom-that-encourages, not bliss; sayable beside "Marath" in Swedish and English; not already a brand. Mara → Marath set the pattern: voice first, place derived. Note that Mara itself is Buddhist (Māra, the tempter who rules by delusion), so the counterpart drawn from the same tradition would make the pair native to one source.

| Term | Source and meaning | Fit against the Whisp and the mechanics | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Te / De (德)** | Tao Te Ching — "the Way and its *power*". Te is inner power, virtue, integrity: the Tao as it lives in one particular being, uncontrived. | The closest match to "the Whisp IS the human" — the Way manifested in a person, not a rule imposed. Te is power without force, which is tough love's register. | Very short; derivation must add body. "De" collides with common words in both languages. |
| **Ming (明)** | Tao Te Ching 16 "knowing the constant is called ming (illumination)"; 33 "one who knows others is clever; one who knows himself is ming." | Ming *is* self-knowledge — the Whisp's whole job ("Who am I?"), and the exact inverse of Mara, who is obeyed unseen (S51). Seeing is what empties Mara (S56); ming is the seeing. | Ming dynasty association in English; otherwise clean, two letters from a sayable place name. |
| **Pu (樸)** | Tao Te Ching 28 — the uncarved block; the same chapter says "return to being an infant" and "return to the uncarved block". | A direct hit on S54: the newborn unwritten, then written by experience, then free again. Pu is the state the FIM returns to when Mara dissolves. | The syllable is unusable as a place name in English or Swedish. Keep the *concept* (it belongs in the growth core's lineage), not the word. |
| **Bodhi** | Awakening. In the Buddha's story it is the direct counterpart of Māra: Māra assaults, the earth bears witness, and bodhi follows — under a **tree**. | The source myth's own answer to Mara, and it ends under a tree — the village already has one at its heart (S26). Awakening is what the newborn arc arrives at. | Sacred and widely used (a real place, Bodh Gaya; several brands). Strong meaning, crowded word. |
| **Kalyāṇa-mitta** | Pali: the good / admirable friend. SN 45.2 (Upaddha Sutta): admirable friendship "is the whole of the holy life". The good friend is the one who admonishes. | The Whisp as *friend* rather than voice, and a friend whose defining act is honest correction — tough love in the canon's own words. | Long; derivation would have to take one half ("Mitta") and lose the meaning. |
| **Paññā / Prajñā** | Wisdom — the faculty that sees through Māra's delusion. | The Whisp is "full of wisdom" (S54); this is the word for that wisdom. | Prajñā is heavily used in yoga/wellness naming; Paññā is less so but hard to say. |
| **Sahampati** | Brahmā Sahampati, who asks the newly awakened Buddha to teach, "there are beings with little dust in their eyes" (SN 6.1). | The *encouraging* voice personified — the one who says "go on, it is worth it". | A named deity; odd as a root for a place. Better as an NPC lineage than a name. |
| **Ziran (自然)** | "Self-so", naturalness — being what one is without contrivance. | The adult who is free again as the child was (S54), knowingly. | Unfamiliar sound; the meaning does not survive derivation. |

**Where the fit is strongest (Claude's reading, not a decision):** Ming and Te for the *mechanics* — self-knowledge as illumination, and the Way alive in one person; Bodhi for the *myth* — the same tradition's own counterpart to Māra, ending under a tree. Pu is the concept behind S54 and should be cited in the growth core's lineage whatever name is chosen.

**Not done:** no derivation to a place name (Stefan's, per S65); no check of trademark or domain collisions; no Nordic-echo search (not asked for this round).

**Second pass (2026-09-17, same session) — Stefan's ruling on the first table:** Ming and Te do not work; Bodhi "sounds a bit too round, almost like a child's toy". The bar gains a sound criterion: the word must have *edge* — weight and consonants to stand beside Marath — while still carrying warmth-first, challenge-second.

| Term | Source and meaning | Fit | Sound beside "Marath" | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Kenshō (見性)** | Zen: "seeing one's nature" — the first direct glimpse of one's true nature. | The exact event of S56: seeing what has had you. The Whisp's job named as a verb. | Two hard consonants, clean, angular. Strongest sound of the set. | A fintech carries the name; otherwise little-used. |
| **Zhen (真) / Zhenren (真人)** | Zhuangzi: the *True Person*, the realised, authentic human who is what they are without contrivance. | The FIM made whole (S54) — the destination named as a person, not a state. | Short, strong; "Zhen-" derives well. | "Zhen" is also a common given name; the Zhuangzi lineage is less known than the Buddhist one. |
| **Satori (悟り)** | Zen: awakening, sudden seeing. | Same event as Kenshō, the more familiar word. | Three light syllables; less round than Bodhi, less edge than Kenshō. | Used by several products; familiar enough to feel borrowed. |
| **Vajra** | Diamond / thunderbolt: that which cuts through delusion; Manjushri's sword of wisdom is the same idea. | Tough love as an object — the wisdom that *cuts*. | Hard, two syllables, unmistakable edge. | Heavily used (Vajrayana, many brands); may read as martial rather than warm. |
| **Hongaku (本覚)** | Japanese Tendai: "original awakening" — all beings are awake from the start. | The innate wisdom the Whisp is born full of (S54, challenge 4), and the newborn's freedom. | Strong, ends hard; distinctive. | Doctrinal and obscure; sound is Japanese-specific. |
| **Tathātā** | "Suchness" — things as they are, seen without distortion. | The world seen once Mara's lens is gone. | Soft-strong, rhythmic; the *-th-* echoes Marath without copying it. | Abstract; hard to feel as a place. |

**Claude's reading, not a decision:** Kenshō and Zhen carry both the mechanics and the edge; Kenshō names the *act* (seeing one's nature), Zhen the *result* (the true person). If neither lands, the search may need to leave the Buddhist–Taoist field, or take a Nordic word and let the Buddhist idea sit underneath it, as Mara's Scandinavian lineage sits under the Buddhist one.

**Third pass (2026-09-17, same session) — Stefan's ruling on the second table:** "all words so far sound a bit too Chinese/Asian." The field moves: the *idea* may stay Buddhist–Taoist underneath, as Mara's Buddhist lineage sits under its Scandinavian one, but the *sound* should be Nordic or Western. The bar now: edge, warmth first, not Asian-sounding, and — where possible — a Nordic root a Swedish speaker hears at once.

| Term | Source and meaning | Fit | Sound beside "Marath" | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Vörðr / vård** | Old Norse *vörðr*, "warden": a guardian spirit that follows a person from birth to death. Swedish *vård* = care; the **vårdträd**, the guardian tree of a farmstead, is the same root — a tree beside the house that keeps the family. | Care-first (warmth), guardianship (tough love's protective side), a spirit that walks with you from birth (the Whisp, S39/S48), and a guardian **tree** — the Tree at the village's heart (S26) gets a native lineage for free. | Hard *v*, hard *r/d*: edge, one syllable. Derives easily. | Swedish *vård* also means healthcare / nursing — a place name must step clear of "sjukvård". |
| **Aletheia** | Greek: truth as *unconcealment* — literally *a-lēthē*, un-forgetting (Lethe is the river of oblivion). Heidegger's word for truth as disclosure. | The exact inverse of Mara, whose power is concealment (S51, S56: what has had you, unseen). Growth as un-forgetting what you are — the newborn again (S54). | Four syllables with a *-th-* that answers Marath's without copying it. | Greek, not Nordic; a girl's name and a few brands; philosophy-flavoured. |
| **Fylgja** | Old Norse: the "follower" — a person's attendant spirit, often seen as an animal or a woman, that accompanies them and reflects their nature. | The Whisp as the one who *walks with* you into Marath (S61); reflects who you are (the senses, S18). | Distinctly Norse, edge in the consonant cluster. | The *-lgj-* is hard for non-Swedes; the fylgja in the sagas is more omen than counsel. |
| **Rede / råd** | Old English *rǣd*, Swedish *råd*: counsel, advice, wisdom-in-action (Æthelred "the Unready" = *un-rǣd*, ill-counselled). | The Whisp as *counsel* — tough love is honest counsel. Plain, human, warm. | Short and strong; "Rede-" derives well and sits cleanly beside Marath. | "Rede" is archaic in English; *råd* alone is too everyday in Swedish. |
| **Heill / hel** | Old Norse *heill*: whole, healthy, blessed — the root of *whole*, *hale*, *holy*, Swedish *hel*. | "The FIM is whole" (S54) in one syllable; wholeness, health and blessing as one word. | Edge and warmth together. | *Hel* is also the Norse underworld and its goddess — the word points both ways, which may be a feature or a fault. |
| **Hugr** | Old Norse: mind, thought, courage (Odin's raven Huginn, "thought"; Swedish *håg*). | The inner voice as *mind*; the raven that flies out and returns with what it saw — the Whisp in the Void (S27). | Hard, short, Norse. | Obscure; *hugr* also shades into "desire" and "mood". |

**Claude's reading, not a decision:** *Vörðr / vård* is the strongest — it carries care, guardianship, a spirit that follows from birth, and the guardian tree, all in one Nordic root with edge, and the Buddhist idea (the good friend who guards you honestly) can sit underneath it. *Aletheia* is the strongest *meaning* (un-concealment against Mara's concealment) but the weakest fit to the Nordic sound. *Rede* and *Heill* are the plainest and warmest.

**Not done:** derivation to a place name (Stefan's); trademark and domain checks; a check of how each reads to a non-Swedish ear.

**Fourth pass (2026-09-17, same session) — Stefan restates the brief:** "a name for our 'place 2' where FIMs have a warm and cosy stay and where they can just relax and feel okay to be themselves." Two things follow. (1) *A framing check, not a ruling:* this describes the felt state of the **village and the private home** (S41–S44, far side of place 2) more exactly than it describes place 2 as a whole, which is tendable, woundable (S32, S34) and — per S63 — to be named for a tough-love voice, warmth first and challenge second. Either the brief has shifted from "the Whisp's voice" to "the felt safety", or the name being sought is for the world while the cosiness is the village's. Held as a question for Stefan; the table below takes the whole-world reading and looks for words that carry *both* the safety and the edge. (2) *The bar, cumulative:* Nordic or Western sound; edge to stand beside Marath; warmth first; sayable in Swedish and English; not a brand; and now — the felt sense of being allowed to be oneself.

| Option | Term and root | What it carries | Fit against the Whisp and the mechanics | Sound beside "Marath" | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Option 1 — Frith** | Old English *friþ*, Old Norse *friðr*, Swedish *frid*: peace — but the *kept* kind: sanctuary, protection, the "king's frith"; *friþgeard* / *friðgarðr* = a sanctuary enclosure. The root *prī-* "to love" also gives *free*, *friend* and Frigg. | Peace with oneself (the exact felt state of the brief); a peace that is *kept*, not given — it can be broken and restored, which is the tendable, gardening-not-guarding world (S34); *free* and *friend* in the same root — the branches (S36–38) and the newborn made free again (S54). | The Whisp as the voice that makes peace with who you are, and then keeps it honestly; Mara's opposite in the legal sense too: *friðlaus* — outlawed from one's own peace — is what an obeyed Mara makes a person. | Frith / Marath — the same *-th*, one syllable against two, hard *fr-*: the pair sounds like a pair without rhyming. A Swede hears *frid* at once. | An English surname (Frith Street) and an archaic word; English says *th* as in "thin". Little brand load. Derivation is already done — the word is the place. |
| **Option 2 — Trygg** | Old Norse *tryggr*: trustworthy, faithful, safe; Swedish *trygg* / *trygghet* — the everyday Swedish word for exactly "feeling safe enough to be yourself". Root *deru-* "firm as a tree": the same root as *tree*, *true*, *trust*, *troth*. | Trygghet is the brief in one word; the root gives the **Tree** (S26) a native lineage — the place is trustworthy because it is rooted. | The Whisp as the voice you can *trust* — tough love is trustworthy counsel, warmth first. | Hard *tr-*, *gg*: plenty of edge. | The *y* defeats non-Swedes ("trig"); **Trygg-Hansa** is a major Swedish insurance brand — a real collision; needs derivation (*Trygd*, *Tryggheim*) that loses some plainness. |
| **Option 3 — Vard** | Old Norse *vörðr* / Swedish *vård*, the warden — carried from the third pass as its strongest term. | Care and guardianship; the guardian tree (*vårdträd*); a spirit that follows from birth (S39, S48). | The Whisp as warden — care first, protection as the tough side. | Vard / Marath — hard, short, sits well. | *Sjukvård*; *Varda* is Tolkien's; needs a derived form (*Vardh*, *Vardheim*) and the brief's cosiness is weaker in it than in Options 1–2. |
| **Option 4 — Sann** | Old Norse *sannr*, Swedish *sann*: true; *sanning*, truth. Aletheia's meaning (third pass) in a Nordic sound. | "Be yourself" as *be true*; the un-concealment that empties Mara (S56). | The Whisp as the true voice against the deceiving one (S50: Marath "deceiving"). | Short and clean; but a suffix is needed and *-ath* echoes Marath too closely (*Sannath* also reads South-Asian). | *Sanna* is a common girl's name; *Sannheim* / *Sanndal* are the workable derivations. |

**Claude's reading, not a decision:** *Frith* is the first candidate in four passes that meets every criterion at once — Nordic root a Swede hears (*frid*), the felt safety of the brief, an edge and a *-th* that answers Marath, a warmth that is *kept by action* rather than given (so tough love survives in it), and *free* and *friend* underneath. If the cosiness in the brief belongs to the village rather than the world, *Frith* still works for the world and the village could take a derived form (*Frithgard*, the sanctuary enclosure) or keep "the village" (S41). *Trygg* is the most exact Swedish word for the brief but is blocked by Trygg-Hansa and the *y*.

**Not done:** trademark and domain checks; how *Frith* reads to a Danish, Norwegian or German ear; whether Stefan means the world or the village.

**Fifth pass (2026-09-17, same session) — Stefan's ruling on the fourth table:** "Frith sounds like filth." Ruled out. The bar gains a criterion: **no ugly English sound-alike** — the name is heard by English ears before it is read. Same brief otherwise (warm, cosy, allowed to be oneself; edge; Nordic root; sayable beside Marath).

| Option | Term and root | What it carries | Fit against the Whisp and the mechanics | Sound beside "Marath" | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Option 1 — Hugna** | Swedish *hugna*, to comfort, gladden; *hugnad*, solace. From Old Norse *hugga* "to comfort" / *hugr* "mind" — the same family as *hygge* (Norwegian *hugga* → *hygge*) and Odin's raven Huginn. | Comfort and cosiness *from the root that gave the world hygge*, without the worn-out word itself; mind and comfort in one stem. | The Whisp as the voice that comforts by *minding* you — Huginn is the thought that flies out and returns with what it saw (the Whisp in the Void, S27); warmth first, and the mind-root keeps it from being mere softness. | Hugna / Marath: two syllables each, both end in *-a*/*-ath*, hard *g* against hard *r-th*. English ears hear **"hug"** — a kind accident, the opposite of filth. | *Hugna* is literary in modern Swedish; *hugg* (chop) is a partial collision; no brand found. |
| **Option 2 — Traust** | Old Norse *traust*: trust, confidence, **protection, shelter**; the source of English *trust*; the same stem as Swedish *tröst* and German *Trost*, comfort. | Trust, shelter and comfort in one word — the felt safety of the brief, with protection in it. | The Whisp as the voice you can rest your weight on; tough love is trustworthy comfort, not soothing. Consolation presupposes a hurt — which the wounded, tendable world has (S32, S34). | Traust / Marath: hard *tr-*, ends *-st*, real edge. English hears "trust" and "roust"; nothing ugly. | *Trost* is a common German surname; *traust* is unfamiliar to Swedes though *tröst* is transparent; "Traust" may read as a misspelt "trust". |
| **Option 3 — Frid** | Swedish *frid*, Old Norse *friðr* — the same root as the ruled-out *Frith*, in its plain Swedish form. | Peace with oneself; kept peace; *free* and *friend* underneath (fourth pass). | Unchanged from the fourth pass. | Frid / Marath: English ears hear **"freed"** — the newborn made free again (S54). | English readers may see "fried"; *Frida* is a common name; *Fridh* is a Swedish surname. One syllable, may want a suffix (*Fridheim*, *Fridland*). |
| **Option 4 — Grith** | Old English *griþ*, Old Norse *grið*: sanctuary, asylum, truce — and also *home*, lodging; *griðastaðr* = a place of sanctuary; *griðland* = a land of peace. | The sanctuary meaning of *frith* with a different sound; *home* built into the word. | Sanctuary that is also home — the village and the private home (S41–S44) in one root. | Grith / Marath: the *-th* pairing survives; English ears hear **"grit"** — toughness, the tough-love side. | No modern Swedish reflex (a Swede does not hear it); English readers may say "grid"; obscure. |

**Claude's reading, not a decision:** *Hugna* is the strongest — it is the hygge feeling from its own root, the "hug" an English ear hears is a gift, and the mind-stem (*hugr*, Huginn) ties it to the Whisp as the thought that goes out and returns. *Traust* is the tougher of the two and carries shelter; *Frid* is the plainest Swedish and turns *frith*'s problem into "freed". *Grith* is offered for its meaning only.

**Not done:** trademark and domain checks; the world-or-village question from the fourth pass is still open.

**Sixth pass (2026-09-17, same session) — Stefan opens a new field:** "what if we look into Indian culture?" Three observations before the table. (1) The third-pass ruling was against a *Chinese/Japanese* sound (Ming, Te, Kenshō, Zhen); Sanskrit, Pali and Hindi are a different sound field, and one the pair already lives in — **Mara is Sanskrit/Pali** (Māra), so an Indian counterpart makes the two names native to one source, as the first pass hoped. (2) Sanskrit and Old Norse are cousins (both Indo-European), so Indian words often carry a hidden Nordic echo for free — *griha* (house) and Swedish *gård* are one root; *Viveka* is a Swedish girl's name. (3) A caution Stefan should hear once, whatever is chosen: to an Indian ear **"Marath" is Marathi / Maratha / Maharashtra** — a language, a people, a state. Not a blocker for a fantasy name, but it is what 200 million people will hear first.

| Option | Term and root | What it carries | Fit against the Whisp and the mechanics | Sound beside "Marath" | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Option 1 — Abhaya** | Sanskrit/Pali *abhaya*: fearlessness, safety; the **abhaya mudrā** is the raised open hand that says "fear not"; *abhaya-dāna*, the gift of safety, is counted the highest gift. From the Buddha's own story: Māra attacks with fear and doubt, and the answer is *abhaya*. | "Okay to be yourself" as *without fear* — the felt safety of the brief named by what is absent, not by comfort. | Mara works by fear; Abhaya is what its absence feels like. The open hand is the Whisp's gesture — encouragement, not bliss; tough love survives (courage, not cushioning). Same source myth as Mara. | Abhaya / Marath: a-BHAY-a, three syllables against two; the *bh* gives edge. Nothing ugly in English or Swedish. | A given name in India; known in yoga circles (the mudrā); a few small brands. |
| **Option 2 — Sharan** | Sanskrit *śaraṇa*, Pali *saraṇa*: refuge, shelter, sanctuary — *saraṇaṃ gacchāmi*, "I go for refuge", the oldest Buddhist formula; Hindu *sharan* = the shelter one takes at another's feet. | Refuge and shelter — the village and the private home (S41–S44) in one word. | The place one *goes to* for refuge; the ball as the always-one-step-away refuge (S33). | Sharan / Marath: both two syllables with *-ar-* — they sound like a pair. | An extremely common Indian given name and surname; Indians will hear a person, not a place. |
| **Option 3 — Sukha** | Sanskrit/Pali *sukha*: ease, well-being, happiness — literally "good axle-hole", the smooth-running wheel; the opposite of *dukkha*, the suffering Māra feeds on. | Cosiness as *ease* — things running smoothly; at ease with oneself. | Sukha is what a life feels like once Mara's friction is gone — the smooth wheel. | Sukha / Marath: two syllables, soft *s* against hard *m-r-th*. English hears "sooka". | Yoga usage (*sukhāsana*); several wellness brands; soft, little edge. |
| **Option 4 — Maitri** | Sanskrit *maitrī* (Pali *mettā*): loving-kindness, friendliness — from *mitra*, friend. The essence of the *kalyāṇa-mitta*, the good friend of the first pass; Maitreya, the Buddha to come, is "the friendly one". | Friendship as the substance of the warm world — the branches (S36–38) are what it is made of. | The Whisp as the good friend who admonishes; warmth first. | Maitri / Marath: **both begin Ma-** — the two voices as siblings from one syllable. Either a feature (one root, two turns) or too close. | *Mettā* is a named Buddhist practice and heavily used in mindfulness branding; Maitri is a name and an ISRO/other brand; the *ai* is unstable in English. |
| **Option 5 — Viveka** | Sanskrit *viveka*: discernment — the faculty that tells the real from the unreal (Vivekananda, *Viveka-cūḍāmaṇi*). **Also a Swedish girl's name.** | Not cosy: the *seeing* that empties Mara (S56). Edge, not warmth. | Names the Whisp's wisdom precisely; the Nordic echo is real and free. | Viveka / Marath: three against two, hard *v-k*. | Warmth-second at best — fails the brief's cosiness; a common Swedish name. Offered as the tough end of the field. |
| **Option 6 — Alaya** | Sanskrit *ālaya*: abode, dwelling (Him-ālaya, abode of snow); in Yogācāra the *ālaya-vijñāna*, the storehouse consciousness where all **seeds** are kept. | Home; and a storehouse of seeds — the ball that buds seeds (S40) has a lineage here. | The FIM's abode; the place the seeds come from. | Alaya / Marath: soft, open vowels, no edge. | Soft; a few brands; the Yogācāra meaning is doctrinal. Offered for the seed lineage. |

**Claude's reading, not a decision:** *Abhaya* is the strongest — it comes from Mara's own story, names safety as fearlessness rather than comfort (so the tough-love voice survives), has edge in the *bh*, and is clean in both languages. *Sharan* is the cosiest word but is a person's name to a fifth of the world. *Maitri* is worth a moment for the Ma-/Ma- sibling effect. The Indian field fixes the first pass's "not the same source as Mara" gap that the Nordic field could not.

**Not done:** trademark and domain checks; how each reads to Indian FIMs specifically (a native check is worth more than a table here); the world-or-village question stays open.

**Seventh pass (2026-09-17, same session) — Stefan: "eternal rest?"** Read as a meaning-brief for the Indian field, not yet a name. One challenge first: *eternal rest* is death's phrase in English (R.I.P.) and in Buddhism *nirvāṇa* is literally "blown out" — an ending. Place 2 is not an ending: it is grown and tended (S34–35), it can be wounded (S32), the Whisp pushes (S19), and FringeIsland has no end to reach (S60). So "eternal" should mean *undying* — rest that cannot be taken from you — not *final*. With that reading the Indian field yields two words that are not in any earlier pass and that sit on Mara's own name:

| Option | Term and root | What it carries | Fit against Mara and the mechanics | Sound beside "Marath" | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Option 1 — Amara** | Sanskrit *a-mara*: **deathless, immortal, eternal** — the direct negation of *māra*, whose root is *mṛ*, to die (Māra is "the killer", death personified). *Amarāvatī* is the city of the immortals, Indra's heaven — a place already derived from the word by the same voice→place move as Mara → Marath. | "Eternal" in one word, and the word is *not-Mara*. | The warm world named as the negation of the negative voice — the pair Marath / Amara is the pair Mara / not-Mara in the source language itself; the deathless rest is what Mara, the killer, cannot reach (S22: the FIM is never the thing at risk). | Amara / Marath: both carry *-mara-*; three syllables against two; the *a-* prefix is the only difference — which is the point. | A very common name and brand (girls' name in several cultures, Amara Raja, many design and wellness brands); *amara* is "bitter" in Italian and Spanish (Latin *amarus*); Amaravati is a real state capital. |
| **Option 2 — Aram** | Hindi/Urdu *ārām*: **rest, comfort, ease** — "*ārām se*", take it easy; the everyday word for exactly the brief's cosiness. Persian in origin, pan-Indian in use. And: **A-R-A-M is M-A-R-A spelled backwards.** | "Rest" in one word, and Mara reversed. | Rest as Mara turned around — a hidden feature for the discovery-minded FIM; the tough-love side is weaker (ease, not courage). | Aram / Marath: two syllables each, *-ar-* in both, hard *m*; a matched pair to the ear. | Persian, not Sanskrit — the "same source as Mara" argument does not hold; a Biblical region and an Armenian name (Khachaturian); the reversal may read as a gimmick once seen. |
| **Option 3 — Moksha** | Sanskrit *mokṣa*: release, liberation — from *muc*, to set free. The Hindu terminus, and the Jain one. | Not rest but *release* — "the human becomes free again, just like the newborn child" (S54) in the source language. | The growth arc's own destination named; the Whisp's job (S57) is the transfer that ends in moksha. | Moksha / Marath: two syllables, *ksh* gives edge. | Heavily used (yoga studios, a film, brands); a doctrinal terminus — the same "ending" risk as nirvāṇa, softened because release is a state one lives in, not a blowing-out. |
| **Option 4 — Vishram** | Hindi *viśrām* (Sanskrit *viśrāma*): rest, repose, a resting-place (Vishram Ghat at Mathura is where Krishna *rested*). | Rest as a *place* — the word already names resting-places. | The village as the resting-place on the far side (S41). | Vishram / Marath: two syllables, *-r-* and a hard ending; edge from *shr*. | A common given name; *Vishram* is a resting-place in the old sense (a wayside stop), not a home. |

**Claude's reading, not a decision:** *Amara* is the find of the whole journey — the Sanskrit negation of Mara, "deathless", with a heaven already derived from it — and its cost is that the world has used it up as a name and a brand, and Romance ears hear "bitter". *Aram* is the cosiest word and Mara's mirror image, but the lineage is Persian and the trick is a trick. If "eternal rest" is the meaning Stefan wants, *Amara* carries "eternal" and *Aram* carries "rest"; neither carries both, and the pair of them says what the brief said.

**Not done:** trademark and domain checks (Amara especially); the world-or-village question stays open; whether "eternal rest" was a meaning-brief or a reaction to the sixth table — to confirm.

**Eighth pass (2026-09-17, same session) — Stefan clarifies the brief:** "I was looking for never ending warm and safe rest." Held: not rest as an ending but rest that **never runs out** — always there, always warm, always safe. This reconciles with the tough-love voice and the breath-rhythm (S19, cosmology §10) if *never-ending* is read as **availability, not occupancy**: the FIM ventures out and comes back, and the rest is there every time, undiminished — the ball always one step away (S33). The rest is inexhaustible; the FIM is not meant to stay in it. One cultural note: in India rest is sought in *shade*, not warmth (the hearth is the Nordic longing, the cool shade the Indian), so the Indian words for restful safety lean cool; the warmth must come from the icon or the feeling, not the literal sense.

| Option | Term and root | What it carries | Fit against the brief and the mechanics | Sound beside "Marath" | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Option 1 — Ananta** | Sanskrit *ananta*: **endless, infinite**. Ananta (Śeṣa) is the cosmic serpent on whose coils Viṣṇu **rests** in the ocean of milk between the ages — *Anantaśayana*, "reclining on the endless": the icon of a god in never-ending, untroubled, safe rest, from which the world is made anew. | "Never-ending" is the word itself; "rest" is the image every Indian knows it by. | The brief as an icon: rest on the endless, safe, from which creation resumes — world-genesis by gardening (S35) has a lineage here. Availability, not occupancy: Viṣṇu wakes. | Ananta / Marath: three syllables against two, hard *t*, open *a*s; clean in Swedish and English. | *Anant* / *Ananta* is a common given name; resorts and brands; the serpent-bed image needs telling — the word alone says "endless". |
| **Option 2 — Sukoon** | Hindi/Urdu *sukūn*: **peace, calm, contentment, restful ease** — the warmest everyday word in Hindi speech for exactly this feeling ("*dil ko sukoon mila*", the heart found rest). Arabic in origin. | Warm safe rest as a *feeling*, not a doctrine. | The felt state of the village and the home (S41–S44). English ears hear **"cocoon"** — a warm, safe, enclosing rest, and a place one leaves transformed. | Sukoon / Marath: two syllables, long *oo* against Marath's hard ending; soft but not weak. | Arabic, not Sanskrit — no shared source with Mara; a Bollywood-song word; some brand use; the *oo* is a long vowel Swedes will shorten. |
| **Option 3 — Akshaya** | Sanskrit *akṣaya*: **imperishable, inexhaustible, never diminishing**. The *akṣaya-pātra* is the vessel that never empties — food without end. | "Never-ending" as *inexhaustible* — a rest that cannot be used up. | Exactly "availability, not occupancy": the rest is a vessel that refills. The *kṣ* gives edge. | Akshaya / Marath: three against two, *ksh* hard; sayable ("ak-SHAY-a"). | A common given name; the Akshaya Patra Foundation (a large school-meals charity) and the Akshaya Tritiya festival own the word in India. |
| **Option 4 — Kshema** | Sanskrit *kṣema*: **safety, security, well-being, ease**; Vedic *yoga-kṣema*, "gaining and keeping safe" — the Gītā's promise (9.22) "I bring what they lack and keep safe what they have". | "Safe" is the word; the keeping-safe of what one has. | The safekeeping of the ball, the only inviolable thing (S34), and the kept peace of the fourth pass in its Sanskrit form. | Kshema / Marath: two syllables, *ksh* edge. | Hard for Western mouths ("kshe-"); obscure outside Sanskrit; little warmth in the sound. |
| **Option 5 — Amrita** | Sanskrit *a-mṛta*: **deathless** — the nectar of immortality; the same negation as *Amara* (seventh pass) on the other stem of *mṛ*. | Never-ending as undying; nourishment. | Not-Mara again, as nectar rather than as a state. | Amrita / Marath: shares *-r-t-*; three against two. | Amritsar is a city ("pool of nectar"); Amrit / Amrita a common name; heavily branded (Amrita University, foods). |

**Claude's reading, not a decision:** *Ananta* carries the brief most completely — never-ending in the word, safe rest in the icon, and a rest from which the world is remade rather than a rest that ends — and it is clean in both languages. *Sukoon* is the warmest word in the field and "cocoon" is a gift, but it does not share Mara's source. *Akshaya* is the most precise reading of "never-ending" (inexhaustible) but is owned by a charity and a festival.

**Not done:** trademark and domain checks; a native Indian ear on all of them; the world-or-village question — with this brief, the answer is leaning toward *the village and the home are what the world is named for*, but Stefan has not said so.

**Ninth pass (2026-09-18) — Stefan's shortlist by ear:** "I like words like ananta, arran, estros, nostra, innerward and Sunaru." Five of the six are not found words — they are *sounds* Stefan likes, and the pattern in them is clear: open vowels, liquid *r*/*n*, two or three syllables, mostly ending in *-a* or *-u*, no consonant clusters. The journey has moved from finding a word to finding a sound and laying a lineage under it — which is exactly how Marath was made (Mara + *-th*). Reflections, one per word:

| Word | What it is | What an ear hears | Fit | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Ananta** | Sanskrit, endless; the serpent-bed of Viṣṇu's rest (eighth pass). | Warm, rolling, three open *a*s. | The only one of the six with the brief's meaning built in; pairs with Marath as one source (Sanskrit) and one sound family. | A common given name; resorts. |
| **Arran** | A real place — the Isle of Arran, Scotland (and a whisky distillery); the Irish Aran islands beside it in the ear. Gaelic, meaning disputed. | Warm, rolling *-arr-*; the *-ran* is also Rán, the Norse sea-goddess who drowns sailors. | The sound is right, and an island name for a world that came from "FringeIsland" is a nice echo — but a real island is a hard collision for a fantasy world. | Real island, real whisky, Aran sweaters; no meaning a Swede or an Indian hears. |
| **Estros** | Invented. | English ears hear **oestrus / estrus** — the animal heat cycle; also *stress*. | — | The same failure as Frith (fifth-pass criterion): an ugly or wrong English sound-alike. Rule out. |
| **Nostra** | Latin *nostra*, "ours" (feminine). | Most ears hear **Cosa Nostra** first, Nostradamus second. | "Ours" is a beautiful meaning for the commons — the village that is *ours* (S41). | The Mafia collision is immediate and global. Rule out as a name; the *meaning* is worth keeping for the village's epithet. |
| **Innerward** | English compound: *inner* + *ward*. *Ward* is Old English *weard*, a guarded keeping — the same root as Swedish *vård* and Old Norse *vörðr*, the third pass's strongest term. | Transparent English; three syllables ending hard. | The meaning is very good — the inward, guarded place — and it maps onto the ball's *inside* → the private home (S42: centre = inward, rim = outward). But it is a descriptive English word beside an invented one; Marath / Innerward reads as two universes. | "Ward" is also a hospital wing; reads like a label, not a name. Better as the **epithet of the private home** than as the world's name. |
| **Sunaru** | Invented. | *Sun* at the front (warm, to an English or Swedish ear); the whole is Japanese in shape (*suna* = sand; Subaru, Naruto beside it). | Warm, soft, open — the brief's feeling; the softness against Marath's hardness could be a feature: the pair need not match, they are opposites. | Japanese sound (Stefan's own third-pass ruling); no lineage under it; Subaru. |

**Sound-matched derivations from found roots (offered, not proposed):** if the ear wants *Arran*'s shape with a lineage under it, Sanskrit **Araṇya** — forest, wilderness, the place the *vānaprastha* withdraws to for rest and reflection; the *Āraṇyakas* are the "forest texts" — has the *-ar-n-* sound, an Indian source shared with Mara, and a *place* meaning (the forest as retreat, and as something grown — S35). Araṇya / Marath. Caution: a resort brand and a given name. If the ear wants *Sunaru*'s warmth with a source, Sanskrit **Sunanda** (very pleasing, joyful) or **Sundara** (beautiful) carry the *sun-* front with an Indian lineage; both are common names.

**Claude's reading, not a decision:** of the six, *Ananta* is the only one that is both a sound Stefan likes and a word that means the brief; *Arran* is the sound without the meaning (and a real island); *Innerward* is the meaning without the sound, and belongs to the private home rather than the world; *Estros* and *Nostra* fail on sound-alikes; *Sunaru* is warm but rootless and Japanese in shape. If the sound is what decides, *Araṇya* is the found word closest to it.

**Not done:** trademark and domain checks; the world-or-village question is still open — and *Innerward* suggests a way to close it: the world takes one name, the private home another (the inward keeping), the village a third (the commons that is ours).

*Addendum (2026-09-18):* Stefan: "I've seen the logo 'sunaru' on some Swedish cars." Checked: **Sunaru AB** is a registered Swedish company (org.nr 556629-0606, Täby, a building contractor and Maskinentreprenörerna member, with the domain sunaru.se). So the word entered Stefan's ear from a logo, not from a language, and it is a live Swedish company name and domain — ruled out as a name on collision grounds. The sound it stands for (soft, open, *sun-* front) remains a data point for what the ear wants.

*Addendum 2 (2026-09-18):* Stefan: "I think Aranya is also a Swedish company." Confirmed: **Aranya AB** (org.nr 556963-3737, Solna) is a Swedish network and cybersecurity consultancy with the domain aranya.se. The Araṇya derivation offered in this pass is therefore a Swedish trademark and domain collision too. Two lessons for the rest of the journey: (1) the *-a*-ending, vowel-rich sounds Stefan's ear likes are exactly the sounds Swedish companies pick, so every candidate now needs a Bolagsverket / allabolag check *before* it is tabled, not after; (2) a found Sanskrit word with an appealing sound is very likely already taken somewhere — the safer path may be a derived form, as Marath is derived from Mara, since a derivation can be made unique.

**Tenth pass (2026-09-18) — Stefan reopens the East, by sound:** "is there another word that we can use with the same soft sounding from Buddha, Taoist or Shinto and with the same soft feeling as Sunaru or Innerward?" This supersedes the third-pass sound ruling by Stefan's own ask: Japanese and Chinese sources are back in, on condition of *softness* — open vowels, *s/n/m/r*, no clusters. Bar now: soft sound; never-ending warm safe rest / okay to be oneself; sayable beside Marath; no ugly English sound-alike; **checked against Swedish companies before tabling** (quick web check of allabolag/ratsit only — a Bolagsverket search is still owed before any lock). One aside: the Japanese word that *is* the brief — *yasuragi* 安らぎ, peace, restfulness, being at ease — is the name of the Japanese spa at Hasseludden outside Stockholm, so it is taken in exactly the wrong place.

| Option | Term and root | What it carries | Fit | Sound beside "Marath" | Swedish-company check | Cautions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Option 1 — Sunao** | Japanese *sunao* 素直: gentle, unaffected, honest, *being oneself without pretence* — a core virtue in Japanese upbringing, adjacent to Shinto *makoto* (sincerity). | "Okay to be themselves" is the word's own meaning. | The felt state of the home; the Whisp's tough love wants the FIM sunao — undefended. **Su-na-o is almost Sunaru.** | Soft, three open syllables; English hears nothing wrong. | No Swedish AB found (a Japanese SUNAO K.K. exists). | Common Japanese given name; the *-ao* ending is unfamiliar to Swedes (they will say "su-NA-o", which is right). |
| **Option 2 — Samatha** | Pali *samatha*: calm abiding, tranquillity — the *resting* half of Buddhist practice (samatha rests the mind, vipassanā sees). | Never-ending calm as a *practice* one returns to — availability, not occupancy. | The Whisp's other mode: the FIM rests in samatha before the Whisp goes out again; the breath-rhythm (§10). | **Sa-ma-tha / Ma-rath**: shares *ma* and *-th* — the two places sound like siblings, soft against hard. | No Swedish AB found (the UK Samatha Trust exists). | Known in meditation circles; "Samantha" is one letter away and English ears may hear it. |
| **Option 3 — Tokowaka** | Shinto *tokowaka* 常若: "ever-young", perpetual renewal — the principle behind rebuilding the Ise Grand Shrine every twenty years: eternity through renewal, not through permanence. | "Never-ending" the Shinto way — a thing lasts by being tended and remade. | The most exact lineage for **gardening, not guarding** (S34–35): place 2 is never-ending because it is re-grown, and it recedes when untended. | Four soft syllables against Marath's two hard ones; distinctive. | No Swedish AB found (a Japanese cargo ship carries the name). | Long; obscure outside Shinto; the meaning needs telling. Strongest *idea* in the table. |
| **Option 4 — Sumana** | Pali/Sanskrit *sumana*: glad-minded, joyful (*su* + *manas*); also the jasmine flower. | Warmth as gladness of mind; a flower for a grown world. | Soft *su-* front like Sunaru; a name Buddhist texts give to happy laypeople. | Su-ma-na / Ma-rath — *ma* shared. | Not checked in this pass. | A common South-Asian given name; *manas*/Mara are unrelated but sit close in the ear. |
| **Option 5 — Anshin** | Japanese *anshin* 安心: peace of mind, the feeling of being safe and reassured. | "Safe" as a felt state, which is the brief exactly. | The home as *anshin*; the Whisp's presence as reassurance. | Two syllables, soft *sh*, ends *-n*; sits beside Marath without echoing it. | Not checked in this pass. | Widely used in Japanese branding (insurance, banks); less distinctive. |
| **Option 6 — Ikoi** | Japanese *ikoi* 憩い: rest, repose, relaxation — *ikoi no ba*, "a place of rest". | Rest as a *place* — the word already names resting-places. | The village as *ikoi no ba*. | Three vowels, no hard consonant at all — the softest word in the whole journey. | Not checked in this pass. | Possibly too soft to carry a world; English ears hear "ee-koy", odd but not ugly. |
